<margin>♈ <note>The symbol for Aries, the Ram.</note></margin> **I**f the moon is in Aries, it reveals the bride to be beautiful. Much [color?] around the eye, fearing the crowd <note>This likely refers to a modest or shy disposition in public.</note>. This is because Aries is the <term>house of Ares<note>original: "areōs." Ares is the Greek god of war, known as Mars in Roman astrology. A "house" is the zodiac sign over which a specific planet has planetary lordship.</note></term>.

<margin>♉ <note>The symbol for Taurus, the Bull.</note></margin> **I**f the moon is in Taurus, it reveals the bride to be beautiful, fair-skinned, especially at the neck. She is of good character, because Taurus is the <term>house of Aphrodite<note>The goddess of love and beauty, known as Venus.</note></term>. The 13th figure is of such a kind. It is called Populus <note>original: ποποῦλος; Latin for "The People"</note>, which signifies "a crowd." It
<margin>

</margin>
indicates many enemies for him. If you ask about enmity, honor is very slight. If you obtain this regarding a military necessity, he will be plotted against. If it is about a lawsuit, he will suffer. before the inquiry. If it is about enmity, the enemy will be denounced on the following day. Not only will there be peace with him, but also with all enemies. If you obtain this, someone will understand <note>original: καταλάβει; can also mean 'overtake' or 'seize'</note> him.

# PRAYER OF THE STONE-CUTTER

--- Page 25 ---
<meta>This page continues the transition from the previous discussion on geomantic "points" and planetary paths into a structured explanation of the astrological houses. The previous page mentioned the "orderly system" of the heavens; here, the author provides a guide to the twelve "places" or "houses" of the zodiac, which govern different aspects of human life. The author addresses a "dearest friend," suggesting this manuscript was written as a personal instructional manual or a letter.</meta>

...writing, or period which is interpreted cosmically from the beginning. Therefore, my dearest friend, see how they interpret these in the twelve figures <note>original: σχήμασι (schēmasi). In this context, "figures" refers to the twelve divisions of the heavens, known today as astrological houses.</note>.

->**Interpretation of the 12 Figures.**<-

<margin>☉ 1.</margin> The first place <term>place</term> <note>original: τόπος (topos). Astrologers used "places" and "houses" interchangeably to describe the 12 sections of a horoscope.</note> belongs to the zodiac sign and the soul, and the body; it concerns matters of nature, matters of fortune, and the remaining time of a person’s life. In short, it is called the **house of life**.

<margin>☽ 2.</margin> The 2nd place belongs to money, and to the second companion of money <note>likely referring to movable property or liquid assets</note>, and to nourishment and helpers; it is the **house of wealth**.

<margin>☿ 3.</margin> The 3rd place belongs to brothers, and to relatives, and to dual parts <note>possibly referring to bilateral symmetry in the body or "twins"</note>, and inquiries regarding travel; it governs wisdom, things of the left side, and dreams. In short, it is the **house of brothers**.

<margin>♀ 4.</margin> The 4th place belongs to parents and the household, to secrets and fortune, and the completion of a matter. It concerns the father among the three persons <note>this may refer to the "angles" of the chart, or a specific grouping of family members</note>. The father is before all things. It is not only of the father but is also the **house of things below** <note>original: ἀποκάτω πραγμάτων (apokatō pragmatōn). In astrology, the 4th house sits at the bottom of the chart, representing the "foundations" or the "underworld."</note>.

<margin>♂ 5.</margin> The 5th place belongs to children, and to garments, to favorable inquiry, and gladness, and every benefit, and spiritual entry. In short, it is the **house of children**.

<margin>♃ 6.</margin> The 6th place belongs to illnesses, and to servants, and to crafts, and to small four-footed animals, and to those who are careless—likewise of sheep. In short, it is the **house of the sick**.

<margin>♀ 7.</margin> The 7th place belongs to wives and to <unclear>affection</unclear> <note>original: συμπάθειας (sympatheias), potentially "compatibility" or "marriage bonds"</note>, to war, and to the courts, and the hope of an inhabitant.

<margin>♄ 8.</margin> The 8th place belongs to thought and cares, and death and fear, and to affliction, and to the inheritance of the dead. In short, it is the **house of death**.

<margin>☉ 9.</margin> The 9th place belongs to long-distance travel <note>original: στρατείας (strateias), which usually means "military expedition" but here refers to any long journey</note>, to inquiries regarding friendship, and religion, and to laws and prophecy, and the judgment of dreams. In short, it is the **house of dreams**.

<summary>This page defines the first nine of the twelve astrological houses, listing the specific areas of life—such as wealth, family, health, and death—that each "place" governs. It reflects a traditional Hellenistic approach to astrology where each house is given a brief, descriptive name like "house of life" or "house of wealth."</summary>

And they have need of the aforementioned <unclear>equality</unclear> <note>The concept of "Isotes" (equality) was central to Pythagorean friendship.</note> between them, with some <unclear>other</unclear> honor.
From which [come] the [fruits] of much <unclear>learning</unclear>, upon the offerings to <unclear>God</unclear>.
In things which you do not suffer beforehand, and for the idle ones. Preferred <unclear>by</unclear> some for you. Toward the fitting of the parts. But if [there is] fear, or deceit. Therefore <unclear>let us honor</unclear> [?] before the sojourners.
Therefore the name is from <unclear>God</unclear>. The first conception is on the part of the fatherland.
But in the land of all of them. Or if [something] through the common <term>gladiators</term> <note>original: μονομάχων (monomachōn) — literally "single-fighters" or those in one-on-one combat.</note>, or heroes, or kings, or those struggling in general. By each name <unclear>according to</unclear> the circumstances. For with compulsion,

Not by the neglect; that one should have mercy; for it is upon them. That it is necessary from those [things] where friendship is not required, if one were to try. But that it is so for others as well. To be [in] <term>friendship</term>.

<meta>This page transitions from the theoretical discussion of Pythagorean friendship and Christian virtue found on the previous page into a practical manual of onomancy—divination based on the numerical value of names. In the month of <unclear>September</unclear>, note that the day has 12 hours and the night has 12 hours. This is the name of the season from which the whole day is the <term>equinox</term> <note>original: ἰσημερία (isēmeria), literally "equal day."</note>. One must seek how the day increases until June <unclear>23rd</unclear>. On that date, the day has 15 hours and the night has 9 hours. And again, from June, the night increases until December <unclear>23rd</unclear>. Then the night has 15 hours and the day has 9 hours.

And from December until January, the day increases for us again. It is necessary, then, to know the 12 hours throughout the entire year. Each month, day by day, the daylight increases toward 30 [days] 7 [units?]. <note>The Greek text "πρὸς λ ζ̅" is somewhat cryptic; it may refer to a specific calculation of minutes added to the day each month.</note> The Sun has a path which it traverses, like the flow of our breaths, settling the heat. 

Whichever month it may be, whichever month we look upon: for God is the heavenly harbor, who created the dry land. <note>This likely alludes to the Book of Genesis and the separation of land and sea.</note> Therefore, before what appears to those existing in the world, if anything regarding the hours shall be revealed to them, [let it be] as to a faithful friend, and glory be to God.

The text concludes with a theological reflection on God as the creator of the celestial and physical world.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the astrological manual, moving from the theory of planetary humors and elements discussed on the previous page into practical "Hemerology"—the study of the specific qualities of hours and days. This section provides divinations for the first six hours of a day (likely Sunday, given the references to the Sun), predicting outcomes for fugitives, the sick, lost items, and identifying thieves.</meta>

### **Hour 1: Severe.**
The great <unclear>illumination</unclear> of <unclear>the overseer</unclear>.
He who flees from a pursuer: whatever you wish to gain, the matter is not **favorable.**

<meta>This page transitions from a general discussion of solar prognostications (the "Brontologion") into a more specific "Horologion" or "Hourly Guide." This section provides detailed predictions based on the planetary hours of the day, a practice common in Byzantine divination where each hour was governed by a specific celestial body (Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Sun, Venus, Mercury, and the Moon). The scribe moves from general weather and social omens to specific warnings about health, travel, and personal conduct during these specific times.</meta>

# The Hourly Guide of the Seven Planets: The first is of two hours across thirty [degrees]
and every beginning of the twelve zodiac signs. Saturn <note>original: κρόνος (Kronos). In astrology, Saturn is often associated with delay, restriction, and misfortune.</note> likewise occupies two hours according to the same law, it says:

<margin>+</margin>
In these hours, no good work nor action should be undertaken <note>original: πρᾶξιν (praxin). This refers to any purposeful activity, business deal, or ritual.</note>; for who can hold it back from his own hand? But [act only] before it passes. For in this hour, the sick person expects death, especially during the same month. He who commits adultery with a woman in this hour will not remain [with her]; not in her alone [will there be trouble]. If one steals, likewise it might result from the hours. The twelve suns without female influence <note>original: ἀθῆλαιοι (atheliaioi). Likely a technical term referring to "masculine" or "sterile" degrees of the zodiac that lack a "moist" or "productive" feminine quality.</note> <image-desc>three red floret-like decorative symbols arranged horizontally</image-desc> are in it. Such a person will suffer death, especially the one born in this hour. There is a woman among them; if she belongs to their men. In the hour of Saturn <term>Saturn symbol ♄ in red circle</term>, for that of Jupiter <term>Jupiter symbol ♃ in red circle</term> and that of Mars <term>Mars symbol ♂ in red circle</term>, take heed if anything happens regarding the whole action. As for the one who takes [action]: the hour before—

The second [hour] <term>Sun symbol ☉ in red circle</term>: the Sun comes forth; one can achieve nothing. For in this hour, if he who wishes acts for himself, [it is like] a new voice in a mortal hour; he is destined to remain in evils.

Let theft not occur in the "poor" hour <note>original: πενιχρῇ (penichre). Referring to an astrologically "weak" or "void" hour where planetary influence is diminished.</note>, let it not happen; he who knows the poor man will be sick for a long time. But he who has many, many things [will have them] from the poles to the earth. If you wish to attempt a battle, proceed in it; for any change will be harmful, even to the judges.

The hour, whether by day or by night: the one born then will be honest and generous <note>original: ἀληθὴς δὸς (alethes dos). Literally "a true giver," implying a person of integrity and liberality.</note>, having a large head. Let him not be together with the planet <note>original: πλανοῦ (planou). Referring here to a wandering star or a specific planetary alignment.</note>. In these hours, if anyone attempts to trouble someone, it is not a work [of value] but they will perish; they will not leave the road [safely]. He who acts through straight paths will not [encounter] the robbers. <insert>decorative flourish</insert>

<margin>decorative flourish</margin>
The third hour, two [degrees]: <term>Venus symbol ♀ in red circle</term> Venus; and Mercury <term>Mercury symbol ☿ in red circle</term> passes through the twelve signs in it. For the sun <term>Moon symbol ☽ in red circle</term> of the bright hour...

<margin>On the constitution of man</margin>

# On the Constitution of Man

How this [constitution] is, and from which elements [it is made]. From fire, air, earth, and water; that is to say, from the hot, from the cold, from the dry, and from the moist. <note>These "four qualities" were the building blocks of the physical world in ancient science. Every living thing was thought to be a specific mixture of these elements.</note>

<note>technical terms: elements, fire, air, earth, water, constitution of man, hot, cold, dry, moist</note>

<summary>This page contains fragmented divinatory interpretations regarding fire and air before transitioning into a foundational medical-philosophical text on the "Constitution of Man." It defines the human body as being composed of the four classical elements (fire, air, earth, water) and their primary qualities (hot, cold, dry, moist).</summary>
<keywords>elements, constitution of man, fire, air, earth, water, qualities, humors, divination, Greek medicine</keywords>

--- Page 62 ---
<meta>This page continues the transition from the elements of the universe to the elements of the human body, known as the four humors. This "Constitution of Man" provides a framework for understanding health, personality (temperament), and the biological origins of emotions and mental states.</meta>

-> The Finding of the Humors <-

---

<margin>Yellow bile</margin>
## Blood is hot and moist, and it is well if it remains so; phlegm
is cold and moist, resembling the waters; yellow bile is hot
and dry. If it remains so; black bile <note>original: "μελαγχολία" (melancholia), literally 'black bile.'</note> is cold and dry,
resembling the earthy elements. —

## Blood is sweet; phlegm is salty;
yellow bile is bitter; and black bile is acidic in its humor. —

## Among humans themselves, not all are of the same form; from that which 
<margin>The brain</margin>
produces much, comes the <term>pneuma<gloss>the "vital spirit" or "breath of life" believed to circulate through the body to sustain life and thought</gloss></term>. Yellow bile comes from the 
birthplace of the liver; black bile from the internal organs <note>likely referring to the spleen</note>. Phlegm guards the 
parts of the brain.

<margin>Eros <note>Love or Desire</note></margin>
## Not all people have more of this [humor] than
others, but some are prone to illness —

<margin>Of humans</margin>
## Those who have blood in a good
mixture <note>original: "κράσιν" (krasin), the 'temperament' or blending of humors</note> are virtuous; even if they become angry, they quickly cease.

## Those who have yellow bile are prone to outbursts, quick-tempered, and daring.

## Those who are melancholic <note>governed by black bile</note> are men of few words, fearful, and yet lovers of good.

## Those who are phlegmatic are sluggish, pale, and cold. When, 
therefore, the ability to reason <note>original: "τὸ φρονεῖν"</note> is healthy, it is because of the 
good temperament of the brain; for it is neither too hot nor too cold. 
Sorrow, however, arises from coldness and black bile; and madness, 
specifically the affliction of <term>phrenitis<gloss>an ancient term for delirium or inflammation of the brain, thought to be caused by an excess of heat and dryness</gloss></term>, arises from heat and dryness. 
As for the causes themselves, the internal organ is affected by the 
downflow; it is noted that in those who are melancholic, the 
moisture is dried up. They are often the most prudent when they drink, 
for then they possess great wisdom. One should hope for the best, 
lest the blood becomes too "melancholic" and thus easily 
disturbs the mind. That which is more oily is the poison of the 
affliction of black bile. For when the cold humor is too much in the 
brain, it draws from warm thoughts but brings [distress] 
to the blood. —

## Blood, therefore, is established as the element 
of power; such as in the kidneys. Veins stretch out from them along the back.

<summary>This page describes the four humors—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—defining their physical properties, their origins in organs like the liver and brain, and how they determine a person's character and mental health.</summary>
<keywords>humoral theory, temperament, blood, phlegm, yellow bile, black bile, phrenitis, brain, liver, ancient medicine</keywords>

This is the creative force of heaven; for us, then, the Sun is to our soul what the moist humor is [to the body]. While phlegm is bitter <note>The text here identifies phlegm as bitter, though it was called salty on the previous page; this may refer to a specific pathological state or a different school of medical thought.</note>, it is through the Sun and through heaven—by means of the Sun through the paths of the days—and through phlegm, and through the stages of life, that [this force] exists in all things, even in the matters of desire <note>original: "ἀφροδισιακοῦ" (aphrodisiakou), relating to Aphrodite/Venus or sexual health</note>. It exists through the proportion of the fires.

<margin>full and</margin>

This signifies death. **The twelve [signs] of the Sun [are] two.** They are signs of many diseases. **The Sun is the fourth,** and it is the star of the disease itself; for the one who is ill is helped by its power. For the living being is well-pleasing [to nature]. In all things, our understanding does not [fully grasp] the nature of man. **The fifth [point] is that man, according to the astronomer**, [is measured by] the hours; the Sun... from birth... the time of man is the time of his [bodily] constitution.

**First**, having been placed by the Greatest [Creator]. **Electrum** <note>A natural or artificial alloy of gold and silver</note> is the color... a mixture with lead; and again, the gold which [was mentioned before]...

<summary>This page maps the relationship between the celestial bodies (the Sun, Moon, and five visible planets) and human life, specifically linking each planet to a specific metal and stage of human development. It establishes the "analogy" system where Gold represents the Sun, Silver the Moon, and so on, which is a foundational concept in both alchemy and medieval medicine.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the theological and philosophical exploration of the nature of the Logos (the Word) and the created world. While the previous page focused on Christ as the radiance of God, this page shifts toward a critique of secular philosophy, specifically astrology and "vain reasoning," contrasting human curiosity with divine craftsmanship.</meta>

It belongs to those who wander <note>original: "πλανωμένων" (planōmenōn), a term referring both to the planets (the "wandering stars") and to people led astray by error</note>. By its very <gloss>nature</gloss>, it sees nothing new outside itself. These things have grown naturally from within those who remember the things they have received. But if these things were not foretelling the future through their actual activity, then through them they might persuade us that the whole universe is not the work of nature. Instead, they rely on vain reasonings and fantasies.

There is a vain questioning by those who follow secular learning <note>original: "θύραθεν" (thurathen), literally "from outside the door," a standard term used by early Christian writers to describe pagan or non-Christian Greek philosophy</note>, regarding what sort of days they might encounter, as if I were speaking of mere playthings. If, then, he shall give a certain law to his creatures, being thus fashioned, and if they are changed and released, they hasten toward a certain harsher anger of their own. An investigation reveals nothing new in the fact that he fashions the thought through various arts. And so, leave the thought to its own traces. 

Yet, it is not the one who fashioned it. If he is wise—<margin>note this</margin>—she appeared. But if he is wise in folly here, for not even upon the one thing does he find new things for those within it, <gloss>not even</gloss> after having scrutinized it minutely <note>original: "ἐξονυχίσας" (exonuchisas), a vivid term meaning to "scrape with the nail," implying a microscopic or overly detailed examination</note>. And in making a defense for such things, they speak very much to the earth; these are the <term>astrologers</term> <note>In this context, "astrologoi" refers to those who believe human fate is determined by the stars, a practice often condemned by theological writers of this period.</note>. And the <term>Chaldaeans</term> <note>Ancient Mesopotamians famous for their astronomical and occult knowledge; the term became a synonym for soothsayers or magicians.</note>, who are useless in these matters. They are like husks alongside the mind, trying to guard the reasoning of one who has died. 

But even if this comes from our joy, if it is the will of a metaphorical God, through whom they have crafted something wise, he thus transforms the inner thought. But things leading to vanity are not so. If indeed I see things of old, this meddlesome curiosity <note>original: "πολυπραγμονία" (polypragmonia), meaning excessive curiosity or meddling in things beyond one's concern</note> is a denial, and those of the Word (Logos) do not feel that sting; nevertheless, I have said it. Because of this matter lying before us, the sculptor <note>original: "λαξευτήρ" (laxeutēr), a stone-cutter or carver</note>, because he carved you toward the beautiful, you might find that the matter is transformed among empty things before it happens. And they come in great numbers, having been angered like a passing year. But if they should see from the pine tree of those people, they would see the destiny that has flown away. But it desires and does not overflow.

<summary>This page presents a critique of pagan astrology and "outside" philosophy, arguing that such meddlesome curiosity (polypragmonia) fails to grasp the true nature of the divine Creator. It contrasts the "vain reasonings" of the Chaldaeans with the purposeful "crafting" of the Logos who directs the soul toward the beautiful.</summary>

<keywords>astrology, Chaldaeans, Logos, polypragmonia, creation, secular philosophy, divine craftsmanship</keywords>

# The Fortune of Venus :: ♀

<margin><image-desc>Geomantic figure consisting of four rows of dots: two, one, two, two. (Commonly known as Laetitia, or "Joy").</image-desc></margin>
A matter <unclear>lofhoky</unclear> or for those seeking an outcome <note>original: "ἔξοδος" (exodos); meaning the way out, the conclusion of an affair, or a journey</note>, it is a very useful sign, though quite hunger-inducing <note>original: "πεινατικὸν" (peinatikon); implying a result that may lead to lack or poverty</note>. However, if they are not the ones governing <note>original: "κυβερνοῖσι" (kybernoisi); a nautical metaphor for steering or managing affairs</note>, it is good. Concerning life and friendship, there will be a tribe <note>or family line</note> and increase; it is a good sign. If they rejoice in an outcome, it is a sign of protection. But after much toil, death is good <note>This likely means a "good death" or a peaceful end after a hard life</note>. # 11. Again, concerning the signs of the 12 zodiacal symbols:

<margin><image-desc>A red stylized almond-shaped symbol with a vertical line through its center and two diagonal support lines at the base.</image-desc></margin>
These twelve houses are similar to the twelve houses that the **astronomers** <term>astronomoi</term> <note>original: "ἀστρονόμοι"; in antiquity, this term referred to both observers of stars and those who interpreted their influence, effectively blending what we now call astronomy and astrology.</note> have agreed upon. And so it is from the parts and the rest. And they call the [intention or study] the head.

**The [house] before them, which is the 12th, is in the second part of the month, and one part is in the form of the planet; let it not be another of the occurrences; or even by itself it reveals to us, since it comes from certain astral instruments. And concerning past matters:-**

And through the **twelfth-part** <term>dodekatemorion</term> <note>original: "δωδεκατημορίου"; a micro-zodiac system where each 30-degree sign is divided into twelve 2.5-degree segments, each belonging to a different sign. <margin>12:</margin>
It is a prognostic sign; and they speak of fasting for all people regarding things that are coming to pass; and he spoke of the manner as not giving [authority?] to a house. For indeed, if it is in the present part, and this previously was in the same part; signs lying toward one another for the activity of future things in any of the houses are useless. But if it is in a second sign, it is similarly authoritative in everything. If, again, someone judges or knows if the **oracle** <term>chrēsmos</term> <note>original: "χρησμὸν"; here referring to the divinatory answer or the "lot" drawn through the calculation.</note> appears, they judge it and cannot [change it]; it is not toward a configuration of salvation. ### Concerning the Great Outcome <note>original: "Megale Exodos," referring to the geomantic figure Fortuna Major</note>, and the outcome from that which has arrived:

The Great Outcome is a single step. The shape of the world is [aligned] with that of the <term>meridian</term> <note>original: "mesembrios," referring to the south or the highest point of the sun at midday</note>. The Sun is its visible and intelligible significator. For the outcome is guarded, even from life itself. Nor might they hold much of the time; its significator is the light within. Its rest is good. And in the first house, it is directed. That which comes from it is good, even on the right side: **In the 1st House.** Note: the power is useful; a living being acts alone: **in the 6th sign** <note>In this system, "sign" (semeion) and "house" (oikos) are often used interchangeably to describe the 12 divisions of the zodiac/geomantic chart.</note> the power of wealth and of useful things [prevails].

# The Art of Geomancy

One must know that the components are like a body. Its parts are the pasturings <note>original: "poimánseis", likely referring to the management or "shepherding" of the various lots or figures</note> and all the earth. Because the assembly <note>original: "ekklēsía", meaning church or gathering</note> speaks of the world, you must [know] whatever it says in the world. But know that the earth is the resting place of the righteous; it is necessary also [to know] the four sacred [elements] just as the "long-breathed" ones <note>original: "makropnoíous", a term for those who are long-lived or perhaps those with a "great spirit"</note> and the wicked one. And also the world. It signifies the branches <note>original: "klēmata", also meaning the "climates" or zones of the earth</note> and health with them. **A** matter with truth **2:**

**In the 1st house** <term>Horoscope: the first house of the chart, representing the self and vitality</term> clearly. For indeed, [it signifies] long life. A cause. And the stage of life that is recovering. True glory, the composition of colors.

**In the 2nd** [it signifies] wealth. And love of beauty <note>original: "philanthían", possibly a corruption of "philanthropían" (charity/generosity) or "philokalían" (love of beauty)</note>. Abundance of remaining things. And of giving <note>original: "dóntōn", literally "of those giving," referring to transactions</note>. And the intentions in a matter. And if the matter is altogether good.

**In the 3rd** toward the fathers. Successful understanding. But fear from parents. And the conversation of brothers.

**In the 4th** ancestral [matters] or from inheritance. And in every matter, the time for restoration.

**In the 5th** personal [matters] from your lineage. Long life. You shall receive the matters for him.
<margin>1</margin> And to know the cause.

**In the 6th** it signifies serfs <note>original: "doulopároika", referring to a class of dependent peasants or household slaves</note>, and the lowest vice, and disease.

**In the 7th** concerning women. A father's bride. And it creates friendship and [connections] with a beloved.

**In the 8th** it signifies inheritance and a "good death" <note>original: "euthanasían", meaning a peaceful or noble death</note>. In illnesses, it stands apart from the evils of living things in the matter.

**In the 9th** travel. To stand and meditate in the <unclear>from</unclear> place. Fears and knowledge.

**In the 10th** <gloss>mother</gloss> it signifies the confirmation of a debt. And if there is anything of authority. Acquisition when there is light. And a sealing from them; these are in one branch.

**In the 11th** clearly benefit from a friend. Either good for him, or they <unclear>do</unclear>. Or of the matters...

### Concerning Affliction <note>original: "thlipseōs"; in geomancy, this usually refers to the figure Tristitia (Sorrow)</note> or Destruction. It is a center <term>Kéntron: a cardinal point or pivot in an astrological chart</term>, from... and it is according to the laws.

<image-desc>A large, ornate decorative red initial 'H' featuring an interlaced basket-weave pattern inside the letter forms, accompanied by four groups of three red dots arranged in a diamond pattern to the left.</image-desc>

**Affliction** <note>original: "H thlipsis"</note> signifies hardship for the Jewish people, and a lack of grace for the poor. It possesses the qualities of the upper Mars <note>original: "Arē", the planet of war and aggression</note> and the world, and brings evils to those who live under it. It is associated with those clothed in black <note>original: "melanthytist[as]", perhaps those in mourning or practitioners of dark arts</note> and with flight or exile, murderers, and thieves. It concerns evil tongues and those who are conspicuous; if they bear witness to it, then there is a clear manifestation of evils. Yet, it is near to repentance. It signifies great fear and victims.

**In the 1st house,** it signifies throughout the entire house—as some say—affliction, pain, and grief in life, arising from worldly joy and high-ranking status <note>original: "anō skeu", perhaps referring to the "upper equipment" or honors of office</note>.

**In the 2nd house,** it signifies the affliction of life and [troubles] during the time of a general; and in the life of every general, it signifies dishonor.

**In the 3rd house,** it signifies civil strife, or else [strife] among friends, and through a bad reputation or sin.

**In the 8th house,** [it signifies things] from the present until now: from parents, and from the weeping of mothers; it signifies many inheritances for the son from evil men, and [troubles from] a general of friends.

**In the 6th house,** as a house it signifies the evils of life; remaining in a house of evils with the rest of the wicked.

**In the 9th house,** it signifies sickness, affliction, pain, and uselessness or poverty, and the threat of murder.

**In the 7th house,** it signifies adulterous women or men, [corrupt] rulers, thieves, and disorder among them; also battles and sickness within that house.

### Concerning this

of this which happens in such a way! For where shall we be known? And the sign itself of the 4 | 2 <note>original: δ' | β'; this numerical shorthand refers to the arrangement of points in a geomantic figure or its position within the reading.</note> signs. If it should be found above and below according to the head, they will run together with it, both themselves and the sun from a different sign. And the sun signifies it. If it should release that part, it will produce a relaxation there in the 4 | 2 sign. And the thickness of the interweaving of safety <note>original: σωτηρία (soteria); often used in Greek divination to denote 'fortune' or a favorable outcome, similar to the Latin 'Fortuna'.</note>. If it should arrange these things by its own 4th part. For the 4 | 2 sign is indeed the luminary <note>original: φωστῆρα (phostera); a celestial body, usually the Sun or Moon.</note> of it. From what kind of fortune? And from what kind of mother? And with an appearance in the laws from the light before the end, concerning the sun before the earth, often. And the fact that the sun will one day make it disappear because of the son of his wife, concerning the royal rule, or the financial rule, or some other. Because of this, the sign is thus again; this 4 | 2 sign is moved according to the one being taken, whether it is a single movement or not in the 4 | 2. ## The Way ⁚ <image-desc>geomantic figure Via: four single dots in a vertical column</image-desc> ⁚ The Youth ⁚ <image-desc>geomantic figure Coniunctio: two dots, one dot, one dot, two dots in a vertical column</image-desc> ⁚ The Weak One ⁚ <image-desc>geomantic figure Fortuna Minor: two dots, two dots, one dot, one dot in a vertical column</image-desc>

The 5th and 3rd signs; likewise also if "The Way" <note>The geomantic figure Via</note> is there; in the 15th place of the sign; both above and below. ## Concerning the Fortune of Substantial Things

<margin>Concerning</margin>
Let it then be clear that the **Lot of Fortune** <term>Lot of Fortune<note>original: ὁ τῆς τύχης κλῆρος (ho tēs tychēs klēros); a mathematical point in a horoscope used to determine a person’s well-being and material success.</note></term> is able to act for all. From which it signifies. Having set out from these things, Atticus <note>likely referring to an ancient commentator or astrologer</note> even in his craft [says] that whatever the manner is, it is the best concerning practical events. # From the Geomancy: Concerning the body of the adulterer: -

The signs of fire or the signs of water. The oracle <note>original: χρῆσμος; a divine response or prophetic answer</note> is not going to speak.
